tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat >> spec/steering/roadmap.md <<'EOF'\n\n## Step 3 — `get` for values, `show` for anything (2026-10-03)\n\n### Overview\nStep 3 replaces the contract section \"show PATH (step 1 and 2)\" with \"`get` and `show`\". `troupe config get PATH`\nprints a single scalar, which is what `show` used to do. A mapping or list gives exit 5, and stderr mentions\n`config show`. `troupe config show [PATH]` prints a scalar like `get`, and a mapping, list or the whole\nconfiguration (PATH omitted) as block-style YAML with includes resolved and text includes as literal blocks.\n`--sources` adds `# from <file>` (relative to the theatre directory, `/` separators) to every line that holds a\nscalar. Decision A (files are the truth) and reconcile-first (step 2) apply to both commands.\n\n### Approach Decision\n- **Chosen**: our own small block-style emitter over the source-mapped `ConfigNode` tree. Each output line\n  records the scalar it holds, so `--sources` comments are attached when the line is built. Scalar, key and\n  literal-block rendering reuses config-set-writeback's `scalar_render`/`block_scalar` rules, moved to a\n  write-neutral shared home if needed. The output must load back to the same configuration.\n- **Why**: the loader already records the file every node comes from (`origin.file`, and text includes point at\n  the included file), so provenance costs nothing. Owning the emitter gives exact control over which line\n  carries which comment, and keeps scalar typing aligned with our YAML 1.2 loader.\n- **Rejected alternatives**: ruamel dump and then matching lines to values to insert comments (fragile);\n  ruamel `CommentedMap` with eol comments (ruamel decides columns, widths, float forms and quoting, and needs a\n  second tree); printing source spans (no include resolution, not block style).\n\n### Existing Spec Updates\n- [ ] config-foundation -- Requirement 6 (\"show a value\") becomes `get PATH`; 6.7's exit-5 message also mentions `config ...[truncated 1456 chars]

assistant: Discovery for step 3 is written and committed on `main` as commit `1db2f7e`. Nothing has been built yet: this step only produces the plan. Running it to completion means the specify-and-implement steps below.\n\n**What I decided.** The roadmap already existed, so I added to it. The work is one new spec plus two small updates to existing specs:\n- **New spec `config-get-show`** covers the `get PATH` command, `show [PATH]` printing YAML, and `--sources`.\n- **config-foundation:** its Requirement 6 (the old `show PATH`) becomes `get`, and the exit-5 message also mentions `config show`.\n- **config-file-sync:** `get` joins the commands that reconcile with the files first; `history` still never does.\n\n**Approach.** I chose a small YAML writer of our own that works over the configuration tree the loader already builds.\n- That tree already records which file each value came from, and a text include points at the included file. So `--sources` needs no extra work to find the source.\n- The `# from <file>` comment is added as each line is built, so no lines have to be matched to values afterwards.\n- Scalars reuse the quoting and literal-block rules that `set` already uses.\n- The rule throughout: the printed YAML must load back to exactly the same configuration.\n\nI rejected the ruamel.yaml-based options because ruamel would control comment placement, quoting and number formats, and inserting comments after the fact is fragile. The reasons are recorded in the brief.\n\n**Open choices with my defaults.** These are recorded in the brief, to settle during requirements:\n- `show PATH --sources` on a single value still gets the `# from <file>` comment, printed as YAML.\n- For a literal block, the comment goes on the `|` header line, because a comment can't sit inside the block's content.\n- A text include that a literal block can't hold exactly falls back to a double-quoted string, so the value never changes.\n- Empty sections print as `{}` and `[]`.\n- Indentation is 2 spaces, with one space before `#`.\n- File labels are relative paths with `/`, such as `.troupe/troupe.yml`. An include outside the theatre directory gets a `../` path.\n\nThe discovery process normally asks you to confirm the decision and the approach. Because you asked for the work to be committed in this run, I chose and recorded them without asking; tell me if you want any of them changed.\n\nFiles:\n- `spec/spec/config-get-show/brief.md` (new)\n- `spec/steering/roadmap.md` (new step 3 section; `config-get-show` added to the main spec list; earlier content unchanged)\n\n**Next:** `/method-command config-get-show`, or `/method-command config-get-show` to go straight on. After that, revisit config-foundation and config-file-sync with `/method-command` for the updates above.

assistant: ## Requirements summary\nThe requirements are written in `spec/spec/config-get-show/requirements.md`: six requirements with 40 acceptance criteria.\n\n- **R1 `get PATH`**: prints a single value exactly as step 1's `show` did. A mapping or list exits 5, and stderr names the path, the file and `config show`. Bad command lines exit 2.\n- **R2 `show [PATH]`**: a value prints like `get`. A mapping, list or the whole configuration (no PATH) prints as YAML. `--sources` can come before or after PATH.\n- **R3 YAML layout**: block style with 2-space indents, document order and includes resolved. `{}` and `[]` are used for empty sections. The user's comments, quoting and formatting are not carried over.\n- **R4 Values in the YAML**: the output must parse back to exactly the same values. Strings are quoted only when needed. Text includes and other multi-line strings print as literal blocks (`|`, `|-`, `|+`).\n- **R5 `--sources`**: each line holding a value ends with ` # from <file>`, relative to the theatre directory with `/` separators. A literal block gets the comment on its header line, and a text include names the included file.\n- **R6 Shared behaviour**: both commands reconcile first and never change any file. Errors follow the step 1 conventions, and stdout stays empty on failure.\n\n## Choices I made where the contract is silent\nThese are listed in the Boundary Context and still need recording in `research.md`. The ones you may want to look at:\n- **`show PATH --sources` on a single value** prints it as YAML with the comment, rather than in `get`'s raw form.\n- **Text includes with CRLF line endings, or characters a literal block can't hold,** print as double-quoted strings instead of literal blocks. This departs from the contract's "literal blocks", but otherwise the printed value would differ from the real one.\n- **`get --sources`** is a usage error (exit 2).\n\n## Document status\n- `requirements.md` is written. `spec.json` shows phase `requirements-generated`; the requirements are not yet approved.\n- The changes this implies for config-foundation (Requirement 6 now describes `get`) and config-file-sync (`get` also reconciles first) are noted in the Boundary Context. The existing specs themselves are updated during implementation, in the same change as the code.\n- Committed on `main` as commit `88ebdc5`.\n\n## Next steps\nApprove the requirements or ask for changes, then run:\n```\n/method-command config-get-show     # optional: compare with the existing code\n/method-command config-get-show\n```

The revision is committed on `main` as commit `54634ed`, and the requirements are still unapproved.\n\n## What changed\n- **4.4**: a text include is always printed as a literal block (`|`), whatever its text.\n- **4.5**: if a literal block can't reproduce a text include exactly, such as a file with CRLF line endings, it is still a literal block. I added one exception: a text include that a literal block can't reproduce is excused from the read-back promise. The output still shows the file's exact text, and nothing is changed to make it fit.\n- **4.6, "non-printable characters only as escapes"**: this now applies outside text includes. Inside a text include, such characters appear unescaped.\n\n## Approval\nDo you approve the requirements as revised? Once you do, the next step is:\n```\n/method-command config-get-show\n```

We add a read command and an output renderer to an existing\n  CLI. No new dependency.\n- **Key Findings**:\n  - The loaded `ConfigDocument` already gives every scalar's source file (`ConfigScalar.origin.file`; for a text\n    include this is the included file). `--sources` needs no loader change.\n  - The quoting and literal-block rules `show` needs already exist in `writeback/scalar_render.py` and\n    `writeback/block_scalar.py`. However, `tests/test_core_boundary.py` forbids core modules\n    (`troupe/config/*.py`) from importing `troupe.config.writeback`, and those writeback functions raise\n    `WriteRefusedError`. The write-neutral part must move to a core module that both write-back and `show` import.\n  - A literal block's indentation indicator is relative to the column of the key or dash that owns the block,\n    not to the leading spaces of the header line (verified with ruamel.yaml 0.19.1, below). tool: Write {"content": "# Design Document: config-get-show\n\n## Overview\n**Purpose**: Step 3 splits reading into two commands. `troupe config get PATH` prints a single scalar, as step 1's\n`show PATH` did. `troupe config show [PATH]` prints a scalar the same way, and prints a mapping, a list or the\nwhole configuration as block-style YAML with includes resolved and text includes as literal blocks. `--sources`\nadds `# from <file>` to every line that holds a scalar.\n\n**Users**: developers who read a theatre's configuration, often split across `file:` includes, and need to see a\nwhole section or find the file to edit.\n\n**Impact**: the `show` subcommand moves out of `cli/main.py` into a new command module, next to the new `get`.\nCore gains a node lookup for an optional PATH, and `show_value` becomes `get_value`. The write-neutral scalar\npresentation rules move from write-back to a core module so `show` can use them. Loading, includes, reconcile,\nhistory and `set` behaviour are unchanged.\n\n### Goals\n- `get` behaves exactly as step 1's `show` (1.1, 1.3–1.5), and its exit-5 message also mentions `config show`\n  (1.2).\n- `show` prints any node. YAML output reads back as exactly the printed configuration (4.1), with the\n  contract's one exception: a text include a literal block cannot reproduce (4.5).\n- `--sources` names each scalar's file relative to the theatre directory (5.1–5.5).\n- Both commands keep the step 1 and 2 conventions: reconcile first, files are the truth, nothing written, error\n  shapes (6.1–6.8).\n\n### Non-Goals\n- Preserving source formatting in `show` output (comments, quoting, flow style, number spellings; 3.6).\n- Other output formats, configurable indentation, comment alignment.\n- Any change to loading, includes, path rules, reconcile, `history`, `watch`, or `set`'s output bytes.\n- Anchors, aliases and merge keys (step 6), and Windows.\n\n## Boundary Commitments\n\n### This Spec Owns\n- The `get` and `show` subcommands: argument parsing (opt...[truncated 32369 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-get-show/design.md'; s=open(p).read()\nold=\"    `render_literal`. The header line carries the source; content lines carry none (5.2).\\n\"\nnew=old+(\"  - Reading of 4.4's \\\"two spaces more than the line that holds the block's header\\\": the indentation that\\n\"\n\"    counts is the column of the key or dash owning the header. For `- k: |` that is the key's column, since\\n\"\n\"    content at the dash column + 2 would fall outside the mapping and break 4.1. Verified with ruamel.yaml\\n\"\n\"    (research.md, literal block experiment).\\n\")\nassert s.count(old)==1; s=s.replace(old,new); open(p,'w').write(s)\nimport json;p='spec/spec/config-get-show/spec.json';d=json.load(open(p))\nd['phase']='design-generated';d['approvals']['design']={'generated':True,'approved':False};d['approvals']['requirements']['approved']=True;d['updated_at']='2026-10-03T11:30:00Z'\nopen(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d,indent=2)+'\\n')\nEOF\ngit add spec/spec/config-get-show && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nspec(config-get-show): design get, YAML show and source annotations\n\nshow needs the quoting and literal-block rules that set already has, but core code\nmay not import write-back, and write-back's renderers refuse what show must print.\nThe design therefore moves the write-neutral scalar rules to a core yaml_style\nmodule and adds a small line-based emitter in the CLI. Each line carries the file\nof the scalar it holds, so --sources comments are exact by construction. The\nliteral-indent experiment and every decision are recorded in research.md.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1"}
assistant: ## Status\nThe design is written to `spec/spec/config-get-show/design.md`, with its decision log in `research.md`, and committed on `main` as commit `2f54cd2`. `spec.json` now shows phase `design-generated`; the design is not yet approved.\n\n## Discovery\nI used the light discovery process, since this extends an existing CLI and adds no new dependency.\n\n## Key findings\n- **`show` needs a refactor first.** The quoting and literal-block rules `show` needs already exist in the `set` code. But an existing test forbids core modules from importing that code, and those functions reject some strings with "write refused". So the design moves the parts that don't write files into a new core module, `troupe/config/yaml_style.py`. `set` keeps using them under the same names, and its byte-exact tests must still pass unchanged. This move is the riskiest step and is planned as a separate first task.\n- **`--sources` needs no loader change.** The loaded configuration already records which file every value came from, and for a text include that is the included file. The new YAML writer builds each output line together with the file of the value it holds. Removing the comments therefore gives exactly the plain output (5.5) without extra work.\n- **Literal-block indentation, settled by experiment.** Content must sit 2 columns past the key or dash that owns the block (`- k: |` puts the content under `k`). This is how the design reads the wording of 4.4; reading it more literally would print YAML that doesn't load back correctly. The same experiment confirmed that CRLF content reads back as LF, which is the case 4.5 exempts.\n\n## Review gate\nPassed:\n- Every requirement ID (1.1–6.8) is traced to a component.\n- The boundary sections are filled in.\n- Every component has a file path.\n\n## Next action\nReview and approve the design, then:\n```\n/method-command config-get-show   # optional\n/method-command config-get-show\n```

In the same task it updates every existing test that relied on step 1 `show`, so the suite stays green at every step.\n   - 3.3 adds step 3 notes to the config-foundation and config-file-sync requirements and updates docstrings.\n4. **Validation (4.1–4.5).** End-to-end tests for `get`, `show`, read-back and `--sources`, all four runnable in parallel. Then the full quality gate and a traceability check.\n\n## Quality checks\n- **Plan review gate:** passed. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"YamlStyle: how a string is written as a YAML scalar, and what a literal block can hold.\n\nThis is the single home of the write-neutral scalar presentation rules, shared by write-back\n(``troupe.config.writeback.scalar_render`` and ``.block_scalar``, which import and re-export these\nnames) and by ``show``'s YAML output (config-get-show design, Decision 1). Nothing here refuses a\nwrite: refusals such as lone surrogates in a written file stay in write-back.\n\nA string is written in a *quote style*: plain, single-quoted or double-quoted. :func:`choose_style`\nkeeps a preferred style when it can represent the text; otherwise the minimal style is used: plain\nif possible, otherwise single-quoted, otherwise double-quoted, which can represent every string\n(characters YAML cannot hold literally are escaped by :func:`quote`). No style folds or wraps.\n\nPlain representability is decided by a cheap syntactic check followed by a parse probe: the text is\nplaced in a tiny document of the exact context (block value, flow value, block key, flow key),\ncomposed with the same ruamel.yaml composer the loader uses, and must come back as a plain scalar\nwith exactly the same text, and, for values, typed as that same string by the shared\n:func:`~troupe.config.scalars.resolve_plain_scalar` (so ``0.2`` or ``true`` are never written plain\nas strings). tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; sed -n 200,250p src/troupe/config/yaml_style.py; cat > /tmp/adv.py <<'EOF'\nfrom ruamel.yaml import YAML\nfrom troupe.config.yaml_style import Context, choose_style, quote\nfrom troupe.config.scalars import resolve_plain_scalar\nfrom troupe.config.model import ScalarStyle\ncases=[\"- a\",\"a: b\",\"? x\",\"#x\",\"&a\",\"!t\",\"|\",\">\",\" a\",\"a \",\" \",\"---\",\"--- \",\"...x\",\"... a\",\"--- a\",\"...\",\"%x\",\"%YAML 1.2\",\"true\",\"5\",\"\",\"hello\",\"café\",\"a #b\",\"a\\tb\",\"'a\",\"\\\"a\",\"@a\",\"`a\",\"*a\",\"a:\",\"-\",\"?\",\":\",\"~\",\"null\",\"0x1F\",\"1e3\",\"a\\nb\",\"a\\\\b\",\" a\",\"---a\",\"- \",\"x --- y\",\"=\",\"<<\",\"!!str a\",\"a,b\",\"[a]\",\"{a}\",\"%\",\"﻿a\",\"a\\x85b\",\"a b\"]\ny=YAML(typ=\"rt\")\nbad=0\nfor t in cases:\n    s=choose_style(t,Context.DOCUMENT,None)\n    r=quote(t,s)\n    try:\n        n=y.compose(r+\"\\n\")\n        ok = n is not None and n.tag.value in (\"tag:yaml.org,2002:str\",) or True\n        val = n.value if n is not None else None\n        good = n is not None and getattr(n,'value',None)==t and (s is not ScalarStyle.PLAIN or resolve_plain_scalar(val)==t)\n    except Exception as e:\n        good=False; val=repr(e)[:60]\n    if not good: bad+=1\n    print(repr(t), s.name, repr(r), \"OK\" if good else f\"BAD {val!r}\")\nprint(\"bad\",bad)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/adv.py"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; sed -i '/ok = n is not None/d' /tmp/adv.py; uv run python /tmp/adv.py | grep -v \" OK$\"; uv run python -c \"\nfrom ruamel.yaml import YAML\nfor d in ['=\\n','a b\\n','---a\\n','...x\\n','x --- y\\n','<<\\n']:\n    n=YAML(typ='rt').compose(d); print(repr(d), n.tag, repr(n.value), n.style)\nprint(YAML(typ='safe').load('=\\n'), YAML(typ='rt').load('=\\n'))\n\""}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Source labels of loaded files for ``config show --sources`` (config-get-show Requirement 5.4).\n\nEvery test writes a real theatre, loads it with ``load_config`` and troupe.cli.render import source_label\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.model import SourceFile\n\n\ndef _labels(theatre: Path) -> list[str]:\n    document = load_config(theatre / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n    return [source_label(file, theatre) for file in document.files]\n\n\ndef _file_named(theatre: Path, name: str) -> SourceFile:\n    document = load_config(theatre / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n    matches = [file for file in document.files if file.path.name == name]\n    assert len(matches) == 1, [file.path for file in document.files]\n tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of YamlOutput (``troupe.cli.yaml_output``): block YAML for mappings and lists.\n\nEvery node comes from a real theatre written to disk and loaded with ``load_config`` (the root\nmapping, or a node reached with ``resolve_path``); expected outputs are exact bytes. Read-back\ntests write the output as a fresh theatre's ``troupe.yml`` and load it with the same loader.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.cli.yaml_output import render_yaml\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigDocument\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.resolve import flatten_leaves, resolve_path\nfrom troupe.config.scalars import scalar_equal\n\n\ndef _load(theatre: Path) -> ConfigDocument:\n    return load_config(theatre / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n\n\ndef _show(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, config: str, path: str | None = None) -> bytes:\n    \"\"\"Render the root mapping of ``config`` (or the node at ``path``) without source labels.\"\"\"\n    document = _load(make_theatre(config))\n    node = document.root if path is None else resolve_path(document, ConfigPath.parse(path))\n    return render_yaml(node, label=None)\n\n\n# --- layout -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_nested_mappings_are_printed_in_block_style_in_document_order(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 3.1, 3.2: entries keep document order; nested mappings start on the next line, two\n    spaces deeper.\"\"\"\n    config = \"name: demo\\nagents:\\n    lead:\\n        role: lead\\n        model: big\\n    count: 2\\nz: 1\\n\"\n\n    out = _show(make_theatre, config)\n\n    assert out == (\n        b\"name: demo\\nagents:\\n  lead:\\n    role: lead\\n    model: big\\n  count: 2\\nz: 1\\n\"\n  ...[truncated 10358 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"YamlOutput: render a configuration node as block-style YAML (config-get-show 3.1-3.7, 4.2-4.8).\n\n``show`` prints a mapping or a list (or the whole configuration) with :func:`render_yaml`. Only the\nvalues and their order are printed: the source's comments, quoting, flow style, blank lines and\nnumber spellings are not carried over (3.6). The output reads back with the project's own loader\nas the printed node (4.1).\n\nThe renderer first builds a list of :class:`OutputLine`, each holding the text of one output line\nand the file of the scalar the line holds (``None`` for ``key:`` lines, ``{}``/``[]`` lines and\ndash lines that open a nested collection). It then joins them with ``\\\\n`` plus a final ``\\\\n``;\nwith a labeler, a line holding a scalar gets `` # from <label>`` (design Decision 2), so the output\nwith labels equals the output without them once the suffixes are removed.\n\nLayout (3.1-3.5, 3.7): two spaces per level; ``key: value`` in document order; a non-empty\ncollection value is a ``key:`` line followed by its content two spaces deeper; list items are\n``- `` two spaces deeper than their key (column 0 for a printed list); a collection inside a list\nitem starts on the dash line (compact form); empty collections are ``{}``/``[]``, an empty\nconfiguration ``{}``; no document markers and no trailing spaces.\n\nScalars and keys (4.2-4.8) use the shared presentation rules of ``troupe.config.yaml_style``.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom typing import Final\n\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigMapping, ConfigNode, ConfigScalar, ConfigSequence, SourceFile\nfrom troupe.config.scalars import resolve_plain_scalar\nfrom troupe.config.yaml_style import Context, QuoteStyle, can_represent, choose_style, quote\n\ntype SourceLabeler = Callable[[SourceFile], str]\n\"\"\"Names a loaded file in a source comment (``troupe.cli.render.source_label`` bound to a theatre).\"\"\"\n\...[truncated 5303 chars]

Double quotes are only for characters single quotes cannot hold.\n- 2.3 (review): `yaml_style` (shared with `set`) writes a string containing U+FEFF plain or single-quoted. Our loader and ruamel read it back exactly, but strict YAML 1.2 excludes the BOM from `nb-char`. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"CLI read commands: ``troupe config get PATH`` (config-get-show 1.1-1.5, 6.1-6.3, 6.5, 6.7, 6.8).\n\n``get`` prints the scalar at PATH exactly as step 1's ``show PATH`` did and refuses a mapping or a\nlist with exit 5 and a hint to use ``config show`` (raised by the core ``get_value``). Its parser\nis the standard :class:`~troupe.cli.shell.TroupeArgumentParser`, so a missing or surplus PATH and\nany unknown option (including ``--sources``) are usage errors (exit 2).\n\nThe handler is buffered: on any error stdout stays empty (6.7). The theatre is opened the default\nway, so a never-imported theatre is imported and an imported one is reconciled before PATH is\nresolved (6.2, 6.3); the ``with`` block closes the history store (6.8). Nothing here writes a file\n(6.5): this module imports nothing from ``troupe.config.writeback``.\n\nImports only :mod:`troupe.cli.shell` and :mod:`troupe.cli.render` from the CLI package (never\n``troupe.cli.main``, which imports this module).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport io\n\nfrom troupe.cli.render import format_show_output\nfrom troupe.cli.shell import (\n    Handler,\n    SubcommandRegistrar,\n    TroupeArgumentParser,\n    theatre_option_parent,\n)\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.theatre import get_value, open_theatre\n\n\ndef handle_get(args: argparse.Namespace, buffer: io.BytesIO) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Handler of ``troupe config get PATH``: print the scalar at PATH.\n\n    PATH is parsed before the theatre is opened, so a malformed PATH (exit 2) creates no history\n    state (1.5). tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config show [PATH] [--sources]`` wiring (config-get-show task 3.2), through the real CLI.\n\nProves the CLI behaviour of the new ``show``: a scalar prints exactly as ``get`` (2.1), a mapping\nprints as block YAML (2.2), no PATH prints the whole configuration (2.3), a missing path exits 5\n(2.4), command-line and PATH syntax problems exit 2 (2.5), ``--sources`` on a scalar (2.6) and\n``--sources`` before or after PATH (2.7), plus the registration and the no-write-back import rule\n(6.5). The full byte-exact contract is in the end-to-end modules of tasks 4.2-4.4.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport ast\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nimport troupe.cli.main as cli_main\nfrom support import CliResult, MakeTheatre, RunCli\n\nREPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\nGET_SHOW_SOURCE = REPO_ROOT / \"src\" / \"troupe\" / \"cli\" / \"get_show_command.py\"\n\nCONFIG = (\n    \"# a comment that is not printed\\n\"\n    \"name: demo\\n\"\n    \"count: 3\\n\"\n    \"agents:\\n\"\n    \"  lead:\\n\"\n    \"    name: alice\\n\"\n    \"    prompt: file:lead.md\\n\"\n    \"  helpers: [bob, carol]\\n\"\n)\nFILES = {\".troupe/lead.md\": \"Be kind.\\n\"}\n\nAGENTS_YAML = (\n    b\"lead:\\n\"\n    b\"  name: alice\\n\"\n    b\"  prompt: |\\n\"\n    b\"    Be kind.\\n\"\n    b\"helpers:\\n\"\n    b\"  - bob\\n\"\n    b\"  - carol\\n\"\n)\n\n\ndef _show(run_cli: RunCli, theatre: Path, *args: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"show\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _get(run_cli: RunCli, theatre: Path, *args: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"get\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _assert_ok(result: CliResult, expected: bytes) -> None:\n    assert (result.exit_code, result.stderr) == (0, \"\"), result.stderr\n    assert result.stdout == expected\n\n\n# ---- 2.1: a scalar without --sources prints exactly ...[truncated 6765 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config show`` output reads back exactly (task 4.3; design Testing Strategy, read-back).\n\nFor each fixture the real ``troupe`` console script prints ``show [PATH]`` for a real theatre; its\nstdout is written as a fresh theatre's ``.troupe/troupe.yml`` and loaded with the project's own\nloader (``load_config``, which applies the exact YAML 1.2 core schema; research.md \"Reading back\nin tests\"). The reloaded configuration must equal the printed node of the original (4.1):\n\n- ``flatten_leaves`` gives the same leaf paths, in the same order, with ``scalar_equal`` values\n  (same types, floats by ``repr``);\n- a structural walk of every node (empty mappings and lists included, which leaves skip) gives the\n  same node kinds at the same paths, with each key compared as a typed key: a key written plain in\n  the output is typed by the core schema, so an awkward key printed plain (``true``, ``1.5``)\n  would come back as a non-string and fail (4.8);\n- the reloaded document holds no include at all: YAML includes and text includes were printed in\n  place (3.4).\n\nA section that is a list is wrapped as ``w:`` followed by the unchanged output lines (a block\nsequence may sit at its key's column), since the loader requires a top-level mapping. Fixtures\navoid strings starting with ``file:`` (the loader would read them as includes) and text includes\nholding a carriage return or another character a literal block cannot hold (the approved 4.5\nexception, proven byte-exact by the ``--sources`` and unit tests). A root scalar document is not\nloadable as a theatre and is checked against exact bytes elsewhere.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre, RunCli, TheatreFiles\nfrom troupe.config.constants import CONFIG_DIR_NAME\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\...[truncated 18251 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /tmp && rm -rf rb && mkdir rb && cd rb && cat > chk.py <<'EOF'\nimport subprocess, sys, pathlib\nfrom ruamel.yaml import YAML\ny = YAML(typ=\"safe\", pure=True)\ntroupe = pathlib.Path(sys.executable).parent / \"troupe\"\ncases = [\n (\"l:\\n  - file:t.txt\\n  - k: file:t.txt\\n  - - file:t.txt\\n\", {\"t.txt\": \"  lead\\nx\\n\"}, \"l\"),\n (\"l:\\n  - file:t.txt\\n  - k: file:t.txt\\n  - - file:t.txt\\n\", {\"t.txt\": \"x\\n\\n\"}, \"l\"),\n (\"l:\\n  - - k: v\\n      m: [1, {x: []}]\\n    - []\\n  - {}\\n  - - - deep\\n      - {y: \\\"  y\\\\nz\\\"}\\n\", {}, \"l\"),\n]\nfor i,(cfg, files, path) in enumerate(cases):\n    d = pathlib.Path(f\"t{i}/.troupe\"); d.mkdir(parents=True)\n    (d/\"troupe.yml\").write_text(cfg)\n    for k,v in files.items(): (d/k).write_text(v)\n    out = subprocess.run([str(troupe),\"config\",\"show\",path,\"--theatre\",f\"t{i}\"],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout\n    print(repr(out))\n    a = y.load(out); b = y.load(\"w:\\n\"+out)[\"w\"]\n    print(a == b, a)\nEOF\n/work/repo/.venv/bin/python chk.py"}

Breaking the key typing, the indentation indicator or\nquoting makes it fail.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config show [PATH] --sources`` end to end through the real ``troupe`` console script (task 4.4).\n\nThe comprehensive proof of the source annotations: every line holding a scalar ends with exactly\n`` # from <file>`` (5.1) and no other line does (5.2); the file is the root file, the YAML include\nthe scalar is written in, or the included text file for a text include (5.3); labels are relative to\nthe theatre directory as given, with ``/`` separators and ``..`` only at the start for an include\noutside it, and a relative theatre argument gives the same labels as an absolute one (5.4); removing\nthe suffixes gives the output without ``--sources`` byte for byte (5.5). On the command line the flag\ngoes before or after PATH, and PATH may be omitted (2.7); a scalar with ``--sources`` prints as a\nYAML document of its own with the comment on its line, a literal block header for a text include\n(2.6); a text include that a literal block cannot reproduce (CRLF, a control character) is still a\nliteral block holding the file's text byte for byte (4.5).\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import CliResult, MakeTheatre, RunCli\n\nTHEATRE = \"t\"\n\"\"\"The theatre directory name under ``tmp_path``; ``../shared`` then sits next to it.\"\"\"\n\nROOT = \"\"\"\\\n# root configuration: comments, quoting and number spellings are not carried into the output\nname: \"demo\"\nretries: 5\nflag: 'true'\nnothing:\nagents:\n  lead: file:agents/lead.yml\n  notes: file:notes.txt\n  shared: file:../../shared/p.md\ncrew:\n  - ada\n  - name: bob\n    age: 0x10\n  - []\nempty_map: {}\nempty_list: []\ntexts:\n  crlf: file:crlf.txt\n  bell: file:bell.txt\n\"\"\"\n\nFILES: dict[str, str | bytes] = {\n    \".troupe/agents/lead.yml\": \"name: alice\\nprofile: file:profile.yml\\ntools: [grep, sed]\\n\",\n    \".troupe/agents/profile.yml\...[truncated 9456 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat >> tests/test_e2e_show_sources.py <<'EOF'\n\n\ndef test_labels_keep_a_symlinked_directory_unresolved(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, run_cli: RunCli, tmp_path: Path\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 5.4: an include reached through a symbolic link is labelled with the path as written\n    (`../shared/p.md`), not with the link's target, also when the theatre itself is a link.\"\"\"\n    store = tmp_path / \"store\" / \"deep\"\n    store.mkdir(parents=True)\n    (store / \"p.md\").write_bytes(b\"shared prompt\\n\")\n    (tmp_path / \"shared\").symlink_to(store, target_is_directory=True)\n    theatre = make_theatre(\"prompt: file:../../shared/p.md\\n\", name=THEATRE)\n    (tmp_path / \"alias\").symlink_to(theatre, target_is_directory=True)\n    expected = b\"prompt: | # from ../shared/p.md\\n  shared prompt\\n\"\n    _assert_prints(_show(run_cli, theatre, \"--sources\"), expected)\n    _assert_prints(run_cli(\"config\", \"show\", \"--sources\", \"--theatre\", \"t\", cwd=tmp_path), expected)\n    aliased = run_cli(\"config\", \"show\", \"--sources\", \"--theatre\", \"alias\", cwd=tmp_path)\n    _assert_prints(aliased, b\"prompt: | # from ../shared/p.md\\n  shared prompt\\n\")\nEOF\nuv run ruff format tests/test_e2e_show_sources.py; uv run ruff check tests/; uv run pytest -q tests/test_e2e_show_sources.py 2>&1 | tail -8"}

continue\n    m = re.match(r\"(\\d+)\\. \", line)\n    if cur and m: ids.add(f\"{cur}.{m.group(1)}\")\ndef key(i): return tuple(map(int, i.split(\".\")))\nmods = [\"test_yaml_style\",\"test_source_label\",\"test_yaml_output\",\"test_cli_get\",\"test_cli_show\",\n        \"test_e2e_get\",\"test_e2e_show_yaml\",\"test_e2e_show_readback\",\"test_e2e_show_sources\"]\nfiles = [root/\"tests\"/f\"{m}.py\" for m in mods]\ndef expand(tok):\n    tok = tok.strip()\n    m = re.fullmatch(r\"(\\d+)\\.(\\d+)\\s*[-–]\\s*(?:(\\d+)\\.)?(\\d+)\", tok)\n    if m:\n        a, b, c, d = m.groups()\n        assert c in (None, a), tok\n        return [f\"{a}.{n}\" for n in range(int(b), int(d)+1)]\n    if re.fullmatch(r\"\\d+\\.\\d+\", tok): return [tok]\n    raise ValueError(tok)\ndef cites(doc):\n    head = doc.split(\":\", 1)[0] if re.match(r\"Reqs?\\b\", doc) else \"\"\n    out, other = [], []\n    for seg in head.split(\";\"):\n        seg = seg.strip()\n        if re.match(r\"Reqs?\\b\", seg):\n            body = re.sub(r\"^Reqs?\\.?\\s*\", \"\", seg).replace(\" and \", \",\")\n            for t in body.split(\",\"):\n                if t.strip(): out += expand(t)\n        elif seg: other.append(seg)\n    return out, other\ncovered, bad, notags, n = {}, [], [], 0\ndef scan(path, only=None):\n    global n\n    tree = ast.parse(path.read_text())\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)) and node.name.startswith(\"test\"):\n            if only and not only(node.name): continue\n            n += 1\n            doc = ast.get_docstring(node) or \"\"\n            got, other = cites(doc)\n            if ...[truncated 886 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/review45_trace.py <<'EOF'\nimport ast, re, collections\nfiles = \"test_yaml_style test_source_label test_yaml_output test_cli_get test_cli_show test_e2e_get test_e2e_show_yaml test_e2e_show_readback test_e2e_show_sources test_theatre\".split()\nwant = [f\"{r}.{c}\" for r,n in [(1,5),(2,7),(3,7),(4,8),(5,5),(6,8)] for c in range(1,n+1)]\ncov = collections.defaultdict(list); bad=[]; nodoc=[]; n=0; other=[]\nfor f in files:\n    tree = ast.parse(open(f\"tests/{f}.py\").read())\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node,(ast.FunctionDef,ast.AsyncFunctionDef)) and node.name.startswith(\"test_\"):\n            if f==\"test_theatre\" and not re.search(r\"get_value|node_at\", node.name): continue\n            n+=1\n            d = ast.get_docstring(node) or \"\"\n            head = d.split(\":\")[0] if d.startswith(\"Req\") else \"\"\n            if not head: nodoc.append(f\"{f}::{node.name}\"); continue\n            segs=[s.strip() for s in head.split(\";\")]\n            ids=[]\n            for s in segs:\n                if s.startswith(\"Req\"): ids += re.findall(r\"\\d+\\.\\d+\", s)\n                else: other.append((f,node.name,s))\n            for i in ids:\n                (cov[i].append(f\"{f}::{node.name}\") if i in want else bad.append((f,node.name,i)))\nprint(\"tests\",n); print(\"no-Req-docstring\",nodoc); print(\"unknown\",bad)\nprint(\"gaps\",[w for w in want if not cov[w]])\nfor w in want: print(w,len(cov[w]))\nprint(\"other-spec segs\", len(other))\nfor w in [\"1.5\",\"2.5\",\"6.8\",\"3.6\",\"4.7\"]: print(w, cov[w])\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/review45_trace.py", "description": "Run independent traceability check"}

assistant: **Validation report: config-get-show (feature level)**\n\n**Recommendation: GO.** No critical findings. The two warnings below are low-risk and need no fix before merge.\n\nI did not edit any files. Check results:\n- Full suite: 2628 passed (`uv run pytest -n auto`).\n- `mypy --strict`: clean. `ruff check`: clean. `ruff format --check`: clean.\n- `tests/test_core_boundary.py`: 3 passed.\n- config-set-writeback byte-exact suite (`tests/writeback` and `tests/test_set_*.py`): no changes since c185fb4, 1143 passed.\n\n**E. Cross-task integration: OK (one minor WARNING)**\n- `yaml_output` imports everything it uses from `yaml_style` (`yaml_output.py:49-58`).\n  - One-line strings use `choose_style(value, BLOCK_VALUE or DOCUMENT, None)` (`:211`).\n  - Strings with a line feed that a literal block can't hold are forced to double quotes (`:209-210`), as 4.5 requires.\n  - Keys use `BLOCK_KEY`, then a fallback to single or double quotes when a plain key would read back as a non-string (`:225-238`). This matches the yaml_style docstring, which says keys are compared as text only.\n- `node_at` and `get_value` (`theatre.py:116-143`) both use `resolve_path`. The `show` handler (`get_show_command.py:315-328`) and the `get` handler (`:287-289`) both print scalars through `render.format_show_output`, so 2.1 ("show prints a scalar exactly as get") holds by construction.\n- `source_label` (`render.py:71-79`) plugs into `render_yaml`'s `label` callable through a closure. The contract is consistent.\n- WARNING (acceptable): the literal-block layout exists twice, in `yaml_output._literal_block` (`:179-197`) and `writeback.block_scalar.render_literal` (`block_scalar.py:87-110`).\n  - About 6 lines overlap: body, trailing breaks and padding.\n  - The decisions themselves live in one place: chomping (`required_chomping`) and which characters fit (`literal_characters_fit`) are both in `yaml_style`.\n  - The CLI copy has to differ. It adds the `2` indentation indicator, never refuses, and passes CR through unchanged. `render_literal` refuses those cases, and the CLI may not import writeback.\n  - The design allows this (design.md:416), so I don't count it as a "one decision, one place" violation. A neutral `literal_lines` helper could later move into `yaml_style`.\n\n**F. Requirements coverage: OK**\n- All IDs 1.1–6.8 are named in test docstrings across the new modules. Each maps to code:\n  - 1.x: `get_value` plus the `get` parser.\n  - 2.x: `handle_show` / `node_at` and the `nargs="?"` / `store_true` parser.\n  - 3.x: `_collection_lines`, `_entry_lines`, `_item_lines`.\n  - 4.x: `_scalar_lines`, `_one_line_scalar`, `_literal_block`, `_key_text`.\n  - 5.x: `OutputLine.source` / `_joined`, plus `source_label`.\n  - 6.x: `open_theatre` default plus the buffered handlers.\n- The thinnest coverage: 4.7 is one unit test (`test_yaml_output.py:215`); 6.6 and 6.8 are tested through `show` only (`test_e2e_show_yaml.py:505,563`). `get` uses the same `open_theatre` path, so I accept this.\n- The PO decision "every text include is a literal block, no exception" holds. `_is_literal` (`yaml_output.py:174-175`) returns True for any text include before any character check, and the same `_scalar_lines` serves nested entries, list items and the root scalar (`render_yaml:89-90`).\n- The smoke run confirmed it for a CRLF include nested, in a list item (`- k: |`) and at the root (`| # from .troupe/crlf.txt` followed by `a\r` and `b\r` lines).\n\n**G. Design alignment: OK**\n- Dependency direction holds:\n  - `get_show_command`, `yaml_output` and `render` import nothing from writeback. The only grep hit is a docstring (`get_show_command.py:21`).\n  - `yaml_style` and `theatre` import neither cli nor writeback.\n  - writeback now imports `yaml_style`, which the design newly allows.\n- The writeback modules re-export the moved names via `__all__`, and the moved code is identical to before (e.g. `required_chomping`).\n- Source and test files match the File Structure Plan, with one approved refinement: read-back tests were split into `tests/test_e2e_show_readback.py` (task 4.3). New files `tests/test_cli_get.py` and `tests/test_cli_show.py` are extra unit tests, not drift.\n- Registrar order is `get, show, history, set, watch` (`main.py:75-81`).\n\n**G.5 Boundary audit: OK**\n- Under `src`, only the 8 planned files changed. Loader, store, reconcile, history, watch and the `set` command are untouched.\n- The writeback diff is a pure move plus re-export. Nothing in `tests/writeback` or `tests/test_set_*.py` changed, and they pass. The revalidation trigger is satisfied.\n- Outside `src`:\n  - Edits to `test_cli_set.py`, `test_cli_usage.py`, `test_cli_streaming.py`, `test_e2e_*` are the retargets planned in task 3.2.\n  - `test_e2e_includes.py` was an extra retarget, recorded in the Implementation Notes.\n  - The config-foundation and config-file-sync requirement notes and the roadmap ticks are in scope (task 3.3/4.5).\n\n**H. Implementation Notes: OK (one informational WARNING)**\n- No `_Blocked:_` entries.\n- TAB is single-quoted: this is correct under 4.3's quoting order.\n- U+FEFF written plain or single-quoted is an existing write-back behaviour, deliberately left alone because changing it would change `set`'s output bytes. It's worth a follow-up in write-back, but it's out of this spec's scope.\n- `spec.json` `phase` is still `"tasks-generated"`. It should be updated when the feature is accepted.\n\n**Smoke test (temp theatre, real CLI)**\n- `get name` printed `demo` (exit 0).\n- `get agents` exited 5 with `.../troupe.yml: agents: is a mapping, not a value; print it with `troupe config show agents``.\n- `get name --sources` exited 2.\n- `show` and `show --sources` gave correct output:\n  - Block layout, YAML include inlined, `0o17` printed as `15`, `~` as `null`, flow style turned into block, `"true"` key quoted.\n  - Text include with a leading space printed as `|2+`; `\n`-only include as `|+`; empty include as `|-`.\n  - Labels: `.troupe/troupe.yml`, `.troupe/agents/lead.yml`, `.troupe/crlf.txt`, `../shared/p.md`. Header lines are labelled, content lines are not.\n- `show weird --sources` printed `'--- a' # from ...`, so the root-document quoting works. `show weird` printed the raw `--- a`, the same as `get`.\n- `show nope` exited 5; `show a..b` exited 2 with empty stdout.\n- Reading the output back with ruamel gave the original values. The only difference was the CRLF include, which reads back as `a\nb\n`; 4.1/4.5 allow that exception.\n- No files were created or changed outside the `.troupe` state.\n\nKey files:\n- /work/repo/src/troupe/cli/yaml_output.py\n- /work/repo/src/troupe/cli/get_show_command.py\n- /work/repo/src/troupe/config/yaml_style.py\n- /work/repo/src/troupe/config/writeback/block_scalar.py\n- /work/repo/src/troupe/cli/render.py\n- /work/repo/src/troupe/config/theatre.py

assistant: Step 3 is implemented and committed on `main`. The final check returned GO: the full suite passes (2628 tests, up from 2113 at the start), `mypy --strict` and ruff are clean, and the working tree is clean. Nothing was pushed and no pull request was opened.\n\n## What the commands do now\n- **`troupe config get PATH`** prints a single value exactly as step 1's `show` did. On a mapping or list it exits 5; stderr names the file and PATH and says to use ``troupe config show PATH``.\n- **`troupe config show [PATH]`** prints a single value the same way as `get`. A mapping, a list, or the whole configuration (no PATH) is printed as block YAML with includes resolved. Every text include is a literal block, CRLF ones included, with their characters unchanged.\n- **`--sources`** adds ` # from <file>` to every line that holds a value, including a literal block's `|` header line. The path is relative to the theatre directory with `/` separators, and a text include names the included file.\n- **Shared behaviour:** both commands reconcile first and never write a file, and `history` is unchanged.\n\n## How it went\n- **Process:** each of the 13 tasks was built test-first by its own subagent and reviewed by a fresh, independent reviewer. All 13 were approved on the first review, none needed a debug round, and each is its own commit (`a55f49b` to `69eca9f`).\n- **`set` is unchanged:** the quoting code moved into the new core module `yaml_style` as a pure move. `set`'s byte-exact tests were not modified and still pass (1143 tests).\n- **Final check:**\n  - The fresh full-suite run exited 0.\n  - A smoke run on a real theatre with a YAML include and a CRLF text include gave the expected output.\n  - Every requirement from 1.1 to 6.8 is named by at least one test.\n  - Dependencies point the way the design says, and nothing outside the planned files changed.\n- **Earlier specs and roadmap:** config-foundation and config-file-sync now carry step 3 notes, and the roadmap marks step 3 done.\n\n## Worth knowing\n- **A strict-YAML edge case is left alone.** A string containing U+FEFF (a byte-order mark) is printed without quotes. Our loader and ruamel read it back correctly, but strict YAML 1.2 doesn't allow that character there. `set` has always behaved this way, and fixing it would change `set`'s output, which this spec must not do. It's recorded in the task notes for a later write-back follow-up.\n- **Literal-block layout exists in two places,** in `show` and in `set`. The decisions behind it (chomping, which characters fit) live in one place. The reviewer judged this acceptable under the design.\n- **One subagent restored a file it had broken on purpose with `git checkout -- <file>`.** The file had no uncommitted work, so nothing was lost, and I confirmed `src` had no diff afterwards.
